\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\Sigma_{y_{2}},\Sigma_{y_{3}},\Sigma_{y_{5}},\Sigma_{y_{7}},\Sigma_{y_{3}y_{1}^{5}},\Sigma_{y_{9}},\Sigma_{y_{3}y_{1}^{7}},\Sigma_{y_{11}},\Sigma_{y_{2}y_{1}^{9}},\Sigma_{y_{3}y_{1}^{9}},\Sigma_{y_{2}^{2}y_{1}^{8}}\}.

Corollary 3.32

(
[
40
,
41
]
)
.

One has

(1)

{\mathbb{Q}}[\{\zeta(p)\}_{p\in{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}}({\mathcal{X}})]={\mathcal{Z}}=\mathrm{Im}\,\zeta
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}=\ker\zeta
.

(2)

{\mathcal{Z}}={\mathbb{Q}}1\oplus\displaystyle\bigoplus_{k\geq 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{k}
.

(3)

If
P\in{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}({\mathcal{X}})
, homogenous in weight, then
\zeta(P)
is transcendent number.

Proof.

(1)

Let
Q\in\ker\zeta
such that
\langle Q|1_{{\mathcal{X}}^{*}}\rangle=0
. Then
Q=Q_{1}+Q_{2}
with
Q_{2}\in{\mathbb{Q}}[{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}({\mathcal{X}})]

and
Q_{1}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}
. Thus, reducing by
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}
, one obtains

Q\equiv_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}}Q_{1}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}
and it follows then the expected result.

(2)

As an ideal generated by homogenous in weight polynomials,
\ker\zeta
is graded.
Since
{\mathcal{Z}}\cong{\mathbb{Q}}1_{Y^{*}}\oplus(Y\setminus\{y_{1}\}){\mathbb{Q}}\langle Y\rangle/\ker\zeta\cong{\mathbb{Q}}1_{X^{*}}\oplus x_{0}\mathbb{Q}\langle X\rangle x_{1}/\ker\zeta

then it follows that, as a quotient,
{\mathcal{Z}}
is also graded.

(3)

Let
\xi=\zeta(P)
, where
P\in{\mathbb{Q}}\langle{\mathcal{X}}\rangle
and
P\notin{\ker\zeta}
, homogenous in weight.
Since, for any
p
and
n\geq 1
, one has
{\mathcal{Z}}_{p}{\mathcal{Z}}_{n}\subset{\mathcal{Z}}_{p+n}
then each monomial
\xi^{n}
,
for
n\geq 1
, is of different weight. Thus
{\xi}
could not satisfy
\xi^{n}+a_{n-1}{\xi}^{n-1}+\ldots=0

(with
a_{n-1},\ldots\in{\mathbb{Q}}
). It follows then the expected result.

∎

4.
Conclusion

In this paper, we surveyed our results concerning the renormalization and regularization
of the zeta functions,
\{\zeta(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})\}^{r\geq 1}_{(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}}

via noncommutative symbolic computation and generating series of polylogarithms,

\{\mathrm{Li}_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{r}}\}^{r\geq 1}_{(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}}
, and of harmonic sums,

\{\mathrm{H}_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{r}}\}^{r\geq 1}_{(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{r}}
. This is based mainly on
the combinatorics on the shuffle bialgebras and their diagonal series,
i.e.

{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathop{{}_{{}^{\sqcup\!\sqcup}}}},{\mathcal{D}}_{\;\begin{picture}(220.0,580.0)\put(220.0,592.0){\line{ 0}{-1}{10.0}}\put(220.0,582.0){\line{ 1}{ 0}{20.0}}\put(240.0,582.0){\line{ 0}{ 1}{10.0}}\put(230.0,592.0){\line{ 0}{-1}{10.0}}\put(225.0,587.0){\line{ 1}{ 0}{10.0}}\end{picture}\;}
and
{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
. In particular,
it operated with

(1)

The construction of the pairs of bases (Lie algebra bases and transcendence bases)
in duality (Theorem
2.2
) to factorize the noncommutative rational series
and to get algebraic structure of

\{\zeta(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})\}^{r\geq 1}_{(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})\in{\mathbb{N}}_{\geq 1}^{r}}

by identification of locale coordinates, in infinite dimension (Corollary
3.10
).

(2)

The algebraic structures (Theorems
3.1
,
3.2
and
3.21
)
and the singularity analysis (Theorems
3.7
,
3.16
and
3.29
)
of the polylogarithms and the harmonic sums, for which the global renormalizations has been obtained via
the Abel like theorems, for the pairs of generating series
\mathrm{L},\mathrm{H}
and
\mathrm{L}^{-},\mathrm{H}^{-}
.

In particular, the series
\mathrm{L}
corresponds to the actual solution of (
1.7
),
satisfying the standard asymptotic behaviors,
and the series
Z_{\mathop{{}_{{}^{\sqcup\!\sqcup}}}}
corresponds to the associator
\Phi_{KZ}
.

Nontrivial expressions for Drindfel’d series with rational coefficients,

i.e.

Z^{-}_{\mathop{{}_{{}^{\sqcup\!\sqcup}}}}
and
Z^{-}_{\gamma}
, were also explicitly provided
thanks to various process of regularization via the noncommutative generating series

\Lambda
and
\Upsilon
, which are group-like series, for respectively,

\Delta_{\mathop{{}_{{}^{\sqcup\!\sqcup}}}}
and
\Delta_{\;\begin{picture}(220.0,580.0)\put(220.0,592.0){\line{ 0}{-1}{10.0}}\put(220.0,582.0){\line{ 1}{ 0}{20.0}}\put(240.0,582.0){\line{ 0}{ 1}{10.0}}\put(230.0,592.0){\line{ 0}{-1}{10.0}}\put(225.0,587.0){\line{ 1}{ 0}{10.0}}\end{picture}\;}
(Theorem
3.29
).

(3)

Via the locale coordinates of the power series
Z_{\mathop{{}_{{}^{\sqcup\!\sqcup}}}},Z^{-}_{\mathop{{}_{{}^{\sqcup\!\sqcup}}}},Z_{\gamma},Z^{-}_{\gamma}
and
Z_{\;\begin{picture}(220.0,580.0)\put(220.0,592.0){\line{ 0}{-1}{10.0}}\put(220.0,582.0){\line{ 1}{ 0}{20.0}}\put(240.0,582.0){\line{ 0}{ 1}{10.0}}\put(230.0,592.0){\line{ 0}{-1}{10.0}}\put(225.0,587.0){\line{ 1}{ 0}{10.0}}\end{picture}\;}
,
the regularization maps for divergent zeta were constructed (Propositions
3.13
and
3.24
) over algebraic bases
matching with analytical meaning: on the one hand, the character
\zeta_{\mathop{{}_{{}^{\sqcup\!\sqcup}}}}
corresponds to the regularization,
obtained as the finite parts of the singular expansions of
\{\mathrm{Li}_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{r}}\}^{r\geq 1}_{(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}}
;
on the other hand, the characters
\zeta_{\;\begin{picture}(220.0,580.0)\put(220.0,592.0){\line{ 0}{-1}{10.0}}\put(220.0,582.0){\line{ 1}{ 0}{20.0}}\put(240.0,582.0){\line{ 0}{ 1}{10.0}}\put(230.0,592.0){\line{ 0}{-1}{10.0}}\put(225.0,587.0){\line{ 1}{ 0}{10.0}}\end{picture}\;}
and
\gamma_{\bullet}
correspond to the regularizations obtained as the
finite parts of the asymptotic expansions of
\{\mathrm{H}_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{r}}\}^{r\geq 1}_{(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{r}}
, in different
comparison scales.

(4)

After that, thanks to the Abel like results, we obtained equations bridging the algebraic structures of converging polyzetas

\{\zeta(S_{l})\}_{l\in\mathcal{L}ynX\setminus X}
and
\{\zeta(\Sigma_{l})\}_{l\in\mathcal{L}ynY\setminus\{y_{1}\}}
(Corollary
3.10
) and, by
local coordinates identification, leading to

(a)

the polynomial relations, homogenous in weight, among converging polyzetas
(Table
1
in Appendix),

(b)

the algebraic generators for polyzetas (Example
3.31
),

{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}(X)
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}(Y)
.

Then, using the algorithm
LocaleCordonateIdentification
(partially implemented in
[
7
]
),
the Zagier’s dimension conjecture
[
55
]
holds, up to weight
12
, meaning that
the irreducible polyzetas in
{\mathcal{Z}}_{irr}^{\leq 12}({\mathcal{X}})
(Example
3.31
)
is
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebraically independent (see
[
40
]
, for example, for a short discussion).

(5)

To end, let
l\in\mathcal{L}yn{\mathcal{X}}
such that
l\neq y_{1}
and
l\neq x_{0},x_{1}
. Then one has

(a)

l>y_{n}
and
l>x_{0}^{n-1}x_{1}
,

(b)

\Sigma_{y_{n}}=y_{n}\in\mathcal{L}ynY
and
S_{x_{0}^{n-1}x_{1}}=x_{0}^{n-1}x_{1}\in\mathcal{L}ynX
.

Unfortunately,
\{\Sigma_{y_{n}}\}_{n>1}\not\subset{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}(Y)

and
\{S_{x_{0}^{n-1}x_{1}}\}_{n>1}\not\subset{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}(X)
. Indeed,

(a)

\zeta(2)=\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{2}})=\zeta(S_{x_{0}x_{1}})
is then irreducible (see Example
3.31
).

(b)

By a Euler’s identity about the ratio
23
23

23

See also
[
9
]
, for analogous study
of the ratios
\zeta(s_{1},\ldots,s_{r})/\pi^{s_{1}+\ldots+s_{r}}
.

\zeta(2k)/\pi^{2k},k>1
, one deduces that

\Sigma_{y_{2k}}=y_{2k}\notin{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}(Y)
and
S_{x_{0}^{2k-1}x_{1}}=x_{0}^{2k-1}x_{1}\notin{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}(X)
.

(c)

By Example
3.31
, one obtains
\zeta(3),\zeta(5),\zeta(7),\zeta(9),\zeta(11)\in{\mathcal{Z}}_{irr}^{\leq 12}({\mathcal{X}})
.

(d)

It could remains that, for any
n\geq 1
,
\Sigma_{y_{2n+1}}=y_{2n+1}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}(Y)
and

S_{x_{0}^{2n}x_{1}}=x_{0}^{2n}x_{1}\in{\mathcal{L}}_{irr}^{\infty}(X)
.

In Appendix, we will explain how (
1.7
) arises in Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov differential equations
(KZ_{n})
,
as proposed for
n=3
in

,

]
. Since the work presented in this text is quite extensive
then it will be continued in

]
by treating the case of

, as application of a Picard-Vessiot
theory of noncommutative differential equations (see
[

]
).

5.
Appendix

5.1.
KZ_{3}:
simplest non-trivial case of
KZ_{n}

Let
{\mathcal{V}}
be the universal covering,
\widetilde{{\mathbb{C}}_{*}^{n}}
,
of the configuration space of
n
points on the complex plane,

(5.1)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{C}}_{*}^{n}:=\{z=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n}|z_{i}\neq z_{j}\mbox{ for }i\neq j\}

and the following differential equation over
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{C}}_{*}^{n}})\langle\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{n}\rangle\rangle

(5.2)

\displaystyle{\bf d}F=\Omega_{n}F,

where

\displaystyle\Omega_{n}(z):=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\frac{t_{i,j}}{2{\rm i}\pi}d\log(z_{i}-z_{j}),

so-called
KZ_{n}
equation and
\Omega_{n}
is called universal KZ connection form.

Example 5.1

(trivial case)
.

{\mathcal{T}}_{2}=\{t_{1,2}\}
and
\Omega_{2}(z)=(t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi)d\log(z_{1}-z_{2})
.
A solution of
{\bf d}F=\Omega_{2}F
is

F(z_{1},z_{2})=e^{(t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi)\log(z_{1}-z_{2})}=(z_{1}-z_{2})^{t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi}

and it belongs to
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{C}}_{*}^{2}})\langle\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{2}\rangle\rangle
,

Example 5.2

(simplest non-trivial case)
.

Solution of
{\bf d}F=\Omega_{3}F
can be computed as limit of the sequence
\{F_{l}\}_{l\geq 0}
,
in
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{C}}_{*}^{3}})\langle\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{3}\rangle\rangle
, by convergent Picard’s iteration as follows

\displaystyle F_{0}(z^{0},z)=1_{{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{C}}_{*}^{n}})}

and

\displaystyle\forall i\geq 1,F_{i}(z^{0},z)=F_{i-1}(z^{0},z)+\int_{z^{0}}^{z}\Omega_{3}(s)F_{i-1}(s).

It can be also computed, by another way, by the sequence
\{V_{l}\}_{l\geq 0}
,
in
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{C}}_{*}^{3}})\langle\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{3}\rangle\rangle
, satisfying the following recursion

\displaystyle V_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{(t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi)\log(z_{1}-z_{2})},

\displaystyle V_{l}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle V_{0}(z)\int_{0}^{z}V_{0}^{-1}(s)\Big(\frac{t_{1,3}}{2{\rm i}\pi}d\log(z_{1}-z_{3})+\frac{t_{2,3}}{2{\rm i}\pi}d\log(z_{2}-z_{3})\Big)V_{l-1}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{(t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi)\log(z_{1}-z_{3})}\int_{0}^{z}e^{-(t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi)\log(s_{1}-s_{2})}\bar{\Omega}_{3}(s)V_{l-1}(s),

where
\bar{\Omega}_{3}=(t_{1,3}d\log(z_{1}-z_{3})+t_{2,3}d\log(z_{2}-z_{3}))/{2{\rm i}\pi}
.

With the notations given in Example
5.2
, solution of
KZ_{3}
is explicit as
F=V_{0}G
,
where
V_{0}(z)=(z_{1}-z_{2})^{t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi}
and
G
is expanded as follows

\displaystyle G(z)=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{t_{i_{1},j_{1}}\ldots t_{i_{m},j_{m}}\in\{t_{1,3},t_{2,3}\}^{*}}\int_{0}^{z}\omega_{i_{1},j_{1}}(s_{1})\varphi^{s_{1}}(t_{i_{1},j_{1}})\ldots\int_{0}^{s_{m-1}}

\displaystyle\omega_{i_{m},j_{m}}(s_{m})\varphi^{s_{m}}(t_{i_{m},j_{m}}),

where
\omega_{1,3}(z)=d\log(z_{1}-z_{3})
and
\omega_{2,3}(z)=d\log(z_{2}-z_{3})
and

\displaystyle\varphi^{z}=e^{\operatorname{ad}_{-(t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi)\log(z_{1}-z_{2})}}=\sum_{k\geq 1}\frac{\log^{k}(z_{1}-z_{2})}{(-2{\rm i}\pi)^{k}k!}\operatorname{ad}^{k}_{t_{1,2}}.

One also has

\varphi^{(\varsigma,s_{1})}(t_{i_{1},j_{1}})\ldots\varphi^{(\varsigma,s_{m})}(t_{i_{m},j_{m}})=V_{0}(z)^{-1}\hat{\kappa}_{t_{i_{1},j_{1}}\ldots t_{i_{m},j_{m}}}(z,s_{1},\cdots,s_{m})
.

Moreover, Example
5.2
(equipping the ordering
t_{1,2}\prec t_{1,3}\prec t_{2,3}
), one has

\displaystyle\varphi^{z}(t_{i,3})=\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}\displaystyle\frac{\log^{k}(z_{1}-z_{2})}{(-2{\rm i}\pi)^{k}k!}P_{t_{1,2}^{k}t_{i,3}},

\displaystyle\check{\varphi}^{z}(t_{i,3})=\sum\limits_{k\geq 0}\displaystyle\frac{\log^{k}(z_{1}-z_{2})}{(-2{\rm i}\pi)^{k}k!}S_{t_{1,2}^{k}t_{i,3}},

where
\check{\varphi}
is the adjoint to
\varphi
and is defined by

\displaystyle\check{\varphi}^{z}=\sum_{k\geq 0}\frac{\log^{k}(z_{1}-z_{2})}{(-2{\rm i}\pi)^{k}k!}t_{1,2}^{k}=e^{-(t_{1,2}/2{\rm i}\pi)\log(z_{1}-z_{2})}.

Hence, belonging to
{\mathcal{H}}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{C}}_{*}^{3}})\langle\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{3}\rangle\rangle
,
G
satisfies
{\bf d}G(z)=\bar{\Omega}_{2}(z)G(z)
, where

\displaystyle\bar{\Omega}_{2}(z)=(\varphi^{z}(t_{1,3})d\log(z_{1}-z_{3})+\varphi^{z}(t_{2,3})d\log(z_{2}-z_{3}))/2{\rm i}\pi.

In the affine plan
(P_{1,2}):z_{1}-z_{2}=1
, one has
\log(z_{1}-z_{2})=0
and then
\varphi\equiv\mathrm{Id}
.
Changing
x_{0}=t_{1,3}/2{\rm i}\pi,x_{1}=-t_{2,3}/2{\rm i}\pi
and setting
z_{1}=1,z_{2}=0,z_{3}=s
,

\displaystyle\bar{\Omega}_{2}(z)=\frac{1}{2{\rm i}\pi}\Big(t_{1,3}\frac{d(z_{1}-z_{3})}{z_{1}-z_{3}}+t_{2,3}\frac{d(z_{2}-z_{3})}{z_{2}-z_{3}}\Big)=x_{1}\omega_{1}(s)+x_{0}\omega_{0}(s),

and
{\bf d}G(z)=\bar{\Omega}_{2}(z)G(z)
admits the noncommutative generating series of polylogarithms
as the actual solution satisfying the asymptotic conditions in (
1.11
).
Thus, by
\mathrm{L}
defined in (
3.6
), and the homographic substitution
g:z_{3}\longmapsto(z_{3}-z_{2})/(z_{1}-z_{2})
,
mapping
24
24

24

Generally,
s\mapsto(s-a)(c-b)(s-b)^{-1}(c-a)^{-1}

.

\{z_{2},z_{1}\}
to
\{0,1\}
, a particular solution of

is

To end with
KZ_{3}
, the infinitesimal braid relations
[
18
]
, one has
[t_{1,2}+t_{2,3}+t_{1,3},t]=0
,
for
t\in{\mathcal{T}}_{3}
, meaning that
t
commutes with
(z_{1}-z_{2})^{(t_{1,2}+t_{2,3}+t_{1,3})/2{\rm i}\pi}
and then

(z_{1}-z_{2})^{(t_{1,2}+t_{1,3}+t_{2,3})/2{\rm i}\pi}
commutes with
\mathcal{A}\langle\langle{\mathcal{T}}_{3}\rangle\rangle
. Thus,
KZ_{3}
also admits

(z_{1}-z_{2})^{(t_{1,2}+t_{1,3}+t_{2,3})/2{\rm i}\pi}\mathrm{L}\Big(\displaystyle\frac{z_{3}-z_{2}}{z_{1}-z_{2}}\Big)
as a particular solution
in
(P_{1,2})
. The last expression is quite in the form proposed in
[
18
]
.

5.2.
Computational examples

Due to Corollary
3.10
, by identifying local coordinate in weight with the algorithm

LocaleCordonateIdentification
, we get the following polynomial relations on local coordinates:

Table 1.
Polynomial relations on local coordinates.

\footnotesize\begin{array}[]{crclrcl}\hline\cr&\mbox{Relations}&\text{on}&\!\!\{\zeta(\Sigma_{l})\}_{l\in\mathcal{L}ynY-\{y_{1}\}}&\mbox{Relations}&\text{on}&\!\!\{\zeta(S_{l})\}_{l\in\mathcal{L}ynX-X}\\
\hline\cr 3&\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{2}y_{1}})&=&\frac{3}{2}\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{3}})&\zeta(S_{x_{0}x_{1}^{2}})&=&\zeta(S_{x_{0}^{2}x_{1}})\\

\hline\cr&\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{3}y_{2}})&=&3\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{3}})\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{2}})-5\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{5}})&\zeta(S_{x_{0}^{3}x_{1}^{2}})&=&-\zeta(S_{x_{0}^{2}x_{1}})\zeta(S_{x_{0}x_{1}})+2\zeta(S_{x_{0}^{4}x_{1}})\\

5&\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{2}^{2}y_{1}})&=&\frac{3}{2}\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{3}})\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{2}})-\frac{25}{12}\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{5}})&\zeta(S_{x_{0}^{2}x_{1}^{3}})&=&-\zeta(S_{x_{0}^{2}x_{1}})\zeta(S_{x_{0}x_{1}})+2\zeta(S_{x_{0}^{4}x_{1}})\\

6&\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{3}y_{2}y_{1}})&=&3\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{3}})^{2}-\frac{9}{10}\zeta(\Sigma_{y_{2}})^{3}&\zeta(S_{x_{0}^{2}x_{1}x_{0}x_{1}^{2}})&=&\frac{2}{105}\zeta(S_{x_{0}x_{1}})^{3}\\
